"Far and away the cleverest document in the controversy is Nobili's own defence, to which, although it would carry no conviction to a Protestant or any one acquainted with Indian affairs, there was practically no reply possible from the Standpoint of late medieval Romanism. The temporary cessation of this thirteen years was brought about by the Bull of Pope Gregory on January 31st, 1623, Remanae sedis antists, which gave ecclesiastical sanction to Nobili's system. (A History of Missions in India, p. 64).

The fundamental dishonesty of the Jesuitical system is perhaps revealed in the most striking of all by the remarkable literary forgeries which the Jesuits committed, probably about the middle of the seventeenth century. In order to support Nobili's claim to have restored a hitherto lost Veda, they published — which of the missionaries it was we know not Catholic writers try to exonerate Nobili of any blame in the matter, although the native Christians of Pondicherry always attributed the authorship to him (Mullbauer, p. 179, note 1) — the so-called "Ezour-vedam," "the spiritual teaching," an exposition of so-called natural theology, from which specifically Christian dogmas were eliminated, and the whole of which was arranged in a form pleasing to Tamil taste. The Jesuit missionaries are said to have sworn before an assembly of Brahmans that they had received this book from the god Brahma! In India as in Europe this subtle forgery was for a century and a half regarded as genuine, though the reasons was that neither in the East nor in the West had apy one skilled knowledge of the real Vedas, or a sufficiently developed historical appreciation of them. Protestant missionaries in Madras exposed the fraud about the year 1840. (ibid p. 67. Ezour-Vedam was printed in France at Yverdon in 2 volumes in 1778. See Asiatic Researches, Vol. XIV; pp. 1-159).

His (Nobili's—Br. D. Bh.) letters are left to tell at once of his methods and his gentleness under suffering. Retiring to Ceylon from the storms after forty-two years spent in deception so gross, that he made oath upon a forged scroll before the suspicious Brahmans that he had in very truth sprung from the god Brahma, he died finally in a mud hut near the church of St. Thomas not far from Madras attended by four Brahmans." (The Conversion of India, p. 69).

## Will of Joseph Boden

Extracted From the Principal Registry of the Probate Divorce and Admiralty Division of the High Court of Justice

In the Prerogative Court of Canterbury

In The Name of God Amen. - I, Joseph Boden Late a Lieutenant Colonel in the Honourable East India Company's Service and now about to depart to the Island of Madeira being of sound and disposing mind memory and understanding (praised be Almighty God for the same) do the day and year hereunder written make and ordain this my last Will and Testament in manner and form following (that is to say) I order and direct that all my just debts funeral and testamentary expenses be in the first place fully paid and satisfied. I give and bequeath unto my Aunt Mrs. Anna Maria Torlesse of Bury Street Edmonton in the County or Middlesex Widow the sum of one thousand pounds of lawful money of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Ireland current in England which I request her to accept as a small token of my esteem and affection. Also I give and bequeath unto my Aunt Mrs. Rachael Jackson at present residing in Ossulstoun Street Sommers Town in the said County of Middlesex the sum of two hundred and fifty pounds of like lawful money. Also I give and bequeath unto my Uncle Mr. Henry Torlesse at present residing in Sommers Town aforesaid the like sum of two hundred and fifty pounds of lawful money aforesaid. Also I give and bequeath upto to Robert Parry of Portsea in the County of Hants a Captain in the Royal Navy the sum of two hundred pounds of like lawful money as a small proof of my esteem for him. Also I give and bequeath unto my second cousin Mrs. Hetty Lacey at present resident in Hoxton Square in the said County of Middlesex the like sum of two hundred pounds of lawful money aforesaid. Also I give and bequeath unto my Godson John Boden Morris son of Lieutenant Colonel Morris in the Honorable East India Company's Service on the Bombay Establishment at present residing at the Craven Coffee house in the Strand London the sum of one hundred pounds of like lawful money. Also I give and bequeath unto Eliza Beale the eldest daughter of my esteemed friend Daniel Beale of Edmonton aforesaid Esquire the sum of five hundred pounds of like lawful money which I request her to accept as a proof of the regard I entertain towards herself and parents. Also I order and direct that my executors hereinafter named do and shall invest set apart or appropriate out of my estate and effects (all personal) a sufficient fund for the payment of an annuity or yearly sum of one hundred pounds which 1 hereby give and bequeath to Mrs. Mary Bond at present living at Miss Luntleys Boarding School at Cheshunt street in the County of Herts for and during the term of her natural life to be paid to her by even and equal half yearly payments free and clear of all deductions whatsoever either as respects legacy duty property tax or otherwise the first payment thereof to begin and be made at the end of six calendar months next after my decease. And I do direct that the said annuity of yearly sum of one hundred pounds hereinbefore bequeathed to the said Mary Bond shall be paid into her own proper hands as and when the same shall from time to time become due and payable for her own sole and peculiar use and benefit and independent of her present and any future husband and so as not to be subject to his or their debts control or engagements And that the receipts of the said Mary Bond notwithstanding her present or any future coverture shall be a sufficient discharge to my said trustees and executors for all monies in such receipts acknowledged or expressed to be received And upon the decease of the said Mary Bond and payment of all arrears of the said annuity to the day of her death the sum of money or fund so appropriated or invested to produce the same shall sink into and become a part of the residue of my personal estate hereinafter bequeathed. Also I further direct that my said executors do and shall invest set apart or appropriate out of my estate and effects a sufficient fund for the payment of another annuity or yearly sum of two hundred pounds which I hereby give and bequeath unto my death and respected father Mr. Joseph Boden at present president at Stratford Green in the County of Essex for and during the term of his natural life to be paid to him free from all deductions and abatements whatsoever either as respects legacy duty property tax or otherwise by even and equal half yearly payments the first payment thereof to begin and be made at the end of six calendar months next after my decease. And from and after the death of my said father then I do hereby give and bequeath the said clear annuity or yearly sum of two hundred pounds to Mrs. Margaret Jenkins (known generally by the name of Mrs. Boden and now living with my said father) for and during the term of her natural life to be paid and payable to her by even and equal half yearly payments the first payment thereof to begir aiid be made at the half yearly day of payment next ensuing the death of my said father And from and after the decease of the survivor of them my said father and the said Margaret Jenkins (alias Boden) and payment of all arrears of the said annuity to the day of the death of such survivor I direct that the sum of money so invested or appropriated as last aforesaid shall sink into and form a part of my residuary estate hereinafter disposed of. And I do hereby further order and direct that my said executors do and shall also invest set apart or appropriate out of my said estate and effects a sufficient fund for the payment of an annuity or yearly sum of two hundred pounds of like lawful money free and clear of all deductions as aforesaid and do and shall stand and be possessed thereof Upon trust that they my said executors or the survivors or survivor of them or the executors of administrators of such survivor do and shall pay or cause to be paid the said annuity of yearly sum of two hundred pounds by even and equal half yearly payments (the first payment thereof to be made at the end of six calendar months next ensuing the day of my death) unto and into the proper hands of a natural son of my said father commonly called or known by the mane of John Boden formerly living in some part of Tower Ward London for and during the term of his natural life in case he has not done or shall not do any act deed matter or thing whereby or by reason or by means whereof the same shall be forfeited or become vested in or payable to any Creditor or Creditors of other person or persons whomsoever or that such Creditor or Creditors or other person or persons should attempt to take possession thereof and receive the same and should by any Judgment or Decree of any Court of law or equity be declared to be entitled thereto or if the said John Boden shall not sell assign transfer or set over or otherwise part with or from the said annuity or yearly sum of two hundred pounds or any part thereof unto any person or persons whomsoever but in the event of any such act deed matter or thing Judgment Decree sale Assignment transfer or disposition then and in any or either of such cause I hereby revoke the bequest hereinbefore made of the said annuity and do direct that the sum so invested or set apart to produce the same as aforesaid together with all arrears of the said annuity shall be applied and disposed of in such manner as I have hereinafter directed concerning the same from and after the decease of the said John Boden in case no such act deed judgement decree sale assignment or transfer shall take place and my Will is and I do hereby expressly declare that the receipts of the said John Boden shall be alone a sufficient discharge to my said trustees and executors and the survivors or survivor of them and the executors or administrators of such survivor for so much money as in such receipts respectively shall be acknowledged and expressed to be received. And I do direct that from and after the decease of the said John Boden and payment of all arrears of the said annuity up to the day of his death the sum of money so invested and appropriated as last aforesaid shall sink into an become and be considered part of my residuary estate and effects hereinafter disposed of. And I do hereby nominate constitute and appoint my friends the said Daniel Beale and Westgarth Snaith of Mansion House Street in the City or London Banker and his nephew Thomas Wilkinson the younger of Mansion House Street aforesaid Executors of this my last Will and Testament and I do hereby give and bequeath unto each of them the said Daniel Beale Westgarth Snaith and Thomas Wilkinson the sum of fifty pounds of lawful money aforesaid as a small token of my esteem and as some recompence far their trouble in the execution of this my Will And as to all the rest residue and remainder of my estate and effects of what nature or kind soever and wheresoever not hereinbefore specifically given and bequeathed I give and bequeath the same and every part thereof unto the said Daniel Beale Westgarth Snaith and Thomas Wilkinson their executors and administrators Upon trust that they my said executors and trustees or the survivors or survivor of them his executors or administrators do and shall collect convert into money and realise all my said residuary personal estate and from time to time as the same shall be by them received do and shall lay out and invest the same in their or his names or name in some or one of the public Government or Parliamentary stocks or funds of Great Britain or on real securities in England at interest as they or he in their or his discretion shall think proper (with full power at their or his discretion to alter vary and change the same for other securities of the like nature) and do and shall stand and be possessed thereof in trust to pay assign and transfer the said residuary personal estate trust monies stocks funds securities and promises unto a certain female child said to be begotten by me on and born of the body of Jane Ingram at Bath in the Country of Somerset about three years ago which child if I believe as yet unchristened and at present under the care and protection of Mrs. Mary Ingram formerly of Hambrooke near Bristol and at present living at Ashley Barn Farm near Mr. Hooks Mills not far from Stokes Croft Bristol aforesaid as and when she the said female child shall attain her age of twenty one years And I do direct that in case the said female child shall be under the said age of twenty one years at the time of my decease it shall and may be lawful to and for my said executors and trustees or the survivors or survivor of them his executors of administrators to pay and apply the interest dividends and annual proceeds of the said principal trust monies stocks funds and securities in or upon which such the residue of my estate and effects shall be invested or a competent part thereof at their or his discretion in and towards the maintenance support board and education of the said female child during her minority and to and shall lay out and invest such part of the said dividends interest and annual proceeds as shall not be applied for the purposes aforesaid in the purchase of like stocks funds and securities or on real security as aforesaid and accumulate the same for her benefit And if it shall so happen that the said female child shall not have been Christened at the time of my death I do hereby request that she may be Christened and called Elizabeth after my dear and respected Mother And I do direct that immediately from and after my death she shall at the cost and charges of my estate take assume and use the Surname of "Boden" and I earnestly recommend her to the care and attention of my said executors. But in case the said female child shall happen to depart this life under the said age of twenty one years then I do hereby give and bequeath all and singular my said residuary estate and effects with the accumulations thereof if any and the stocks funds and securities whereon the same shall have been laid out and invested unto the University of Oxford to be by that Body appropriated in and towards the erection and endowment of a Professorship in the Sanskrit Language at or in any or either of the Colleges in the said University being of opinion that a more general and critical knowledge of that language will be a means of enabling my Countrymen to proceed in the conversion of the Natives of India to the Christian Religion by disseminating a knowledge of the Sacred Scriptures amongst them more effectually than all other means what-soever. And I do hereby declare that it shall and may be lawful to and for my said trustees and executors respectively their respective executors and administrators by and out of all or any part of the monies which by virtue of this my Will or any of the trusts herein declared shall come to their or any of their hands to deduct and retain to and reimburse themselves and himself all such costs charges and expanses whatsoever as they shall respectively sustain expend or be put unto to in or about the execution of this my Will or the trusts hereby created or any other matter or thing in any wise relating thereto and that they their executors and administrators shall be charged and chargeable only each and every of them for and with his own receipts payments acts and wilful defaults only and not otherwise and shall not be chargeable with or for any sum or sums of money other than such as shall actually respectively come to his or their hands by virtue of or under this my Will or the trusts hereby created nor with or for any loss or damage which shall or may happen in placing out any of the trust monies as aforesaid on Government or real securities or in the Parliamentary funds or depositing the same or any part thereof in the hands of any Banker or Broker or elsewhere for safe custody or by defect of title or otherwise as regards any security or securities fund or funds to be had or taken in pursuance of this my Will nor with or for any loss or damage which may happen in or about the execution of this my Will or the trusts hereby created without their respective wilful defaults nor shall any or either of them be liable or answerable for any monies for which they or he shall join in any receipt or receipts for the sake of conformity but the part Actually receiving such monies shall alone be responsible for the application thereof and that they my said trustees and executors and the survivors or survivor of them and the executors of administrators of such survivor my adjudge settle compromise and compound all reckonings accounts and transactions depending between me and any other person or persons at the time of my decease in such manner as they or he shall think proper and pay any debt claimed from me upon any evidence which to them or him shall appear satisfactory and their receipt or receipts in writing for any sum of money payable to them or him by virtue of this my Will shall effectually discharge the person or persons to whom the same shall be given from the money therein mentioned to be received and from being accountable or answerable for the loss misapplication or non-application or being in anywise bound to see to the application thereof or of any part thereof And lastly I do hereby revoke all former and other W lls and Testamentary dispositions made by me at any time hertoforc and do declare this to be my last Will and Testament. In Witness whereof I the said Joseph Boden have to this my last Will and Testament contained in seven sheets of paper set my hand and seal that is to say my hand to the first six sheets and my hand and seal to this seventh and last sheet thereof this fifteenth day of August in the year of our Lord one thousand eight hundred and eleven (1811) — Jos.Boden (LS)—Signed sealed published and declared by the said Joseph Boden the Testator as and for his last Will and Testament in the presence of us who in the presence at his request and in the presence of each other have hereunto subscribed our names as witnesses—Henry Sikes, Mansion House Street—Wm Walton Girdlers-hall.

Codicil to a Will made by me Joseph Boden in London dated in August 1811 and deposited with my Executors Messrs Sykes Snaith & Co. In addition to what is contained in that Will I could wish in case of my demise that Mr. James Bowness would immediately present Doctor Meeting of the Portuguese Medical Staff with the sum of Thirty Modores as a small tribute for his kindness to me during my illness. The remainder of whatever property I may be possessed of here together with what I may have in England such as trunks linnen and books I give to my servant William Me Bean of Bombay as his intire property with the exception of two Shawls in England and also a Gold Watch which I at present wear and one Shawl here. These four Articles together with a diamond ring now in the possession of Mrs. Thomas of Mark Lane London I give to the following persons Mrs. Torlesse and her children the watch to her eldest son my namesake and the Shawls to her daughters my cousins Maria and Harriet and the ring to their worthy Mother Mrs. Tor-leasse. The Shawl here I beg Mrs. Thomas of Mark Lane will accept of it with my best regards. I beg the favour Mr. James Bowness will after my decease pay my Servant William Me Bean the sum of twenty pounds being the amount of his wages up to the end of this present year and also procure him a passage to England at the expense of my estate whereon his arrival I beg my executors Messrs Sykes Snaith & Co. will also present him with the sum of one hundred pounds as a reward for his honesty and integrity. Dated in Lisbon this 19th day of November 1811—(Signed) — Jos Boden — Witness (Signed) -John Picket.

I, William Williamson one of the Vice Consuls to the British Nation in the City of Lisbon do hereby Certify the above and foregoing to be a true and faithful copy extracted from the Register in this Vice-Consulate of a written Paper or Codicil presented to me by Mr. James Bowness a Merchant established in this City who declared the same to have been written by him at the particular request and agreeably to what had been dictated to him by Jos: Boden Esqr who departed this life in this City on the twenty-first day of the present month November about the hour of One O'clock in the afternoon and who had signed the same in his presence and in that of the Witness thereunto subscribed which was confirmed to me by the said Witness. The said written paper or Codicil having been presented to me in a cover sealed with wafer and with the superscription "To Mr. James Bowness Lisbon to be opened in case of my demise" I opened it by the desire of Mr. Bowness and read the contents to him and the Witness aforesaid which contents are in strict conformity to the foregoing copy. In M.P. Testimony of all which I have hereunto set my hand and affixed the Seal of this Vice Consulate at Lisbon the twenty third day of November one thousand eight hundred and eleven—Wm Williamson Vice Consul. (LS)

Proved (with a Codicil) 17th December, 1811. Fos44HJ.T.

I Certify that this copy has been examined with the original Will and official copy of the original Codicil deposited in this Registry and that it is a true copy thereof.

Registrar.

## A dialogue Between a Christian Missionary and Three Chinese Converts

Missionary. How many Gods are there, my brethren!

1st Convert. Three.

2nd Convert. Two.

3rd Convert. None.

Missionary. Horrid! The answers are from the Devil.

All. We know not where you got the religion which you have taught us, but thus you have taught us. Missionary. Blasphemers! All. We have heard you with patience nor ever thought of crying out against you, how much so ever you surprised us by your doctrine. Missionary. (Recovering himself and addressing the 1st convert)

Come, come, recollect: how can you imagine that there are three Gods? 1st Convert. You told me there was God the Father, and God the Son, and God the Holy Ghost, and by my Swanpan I find that one ad one and one are three. Missionary. O! I see your blunder. You remember but half the lesson. I told you also that these Three are One. 1st Convert. I know you did, but I thought you had forgotten yourself, and concluded that you spoke the truth first. Missionary. O no! You must believe not only that there are Three persons, each God, and equal in power and glory, but also, that these Three are One. 1st Convert. That is impossible. In China we do not believe contradictions.

Missionary. Brother! It is a mystery. 1st Convert. What is that, pray? Missionary. It is — it is — I know not what to say to you, except that it is something which you cannot possibly comprehend.

1st Convert. (Smiling) And is it this that you have been sent 10,000 miles to teach?

Missionary. O the power of carnal reason! Surely, some Socinian has been doing the Devil's work in China. But (turning to the 2nd convert) how could you imagine, there are two Gods.

2nd Convert. I thought there were many more till you came and lessened the number.

Missionary. Have I ever told you that there are two Gods? (Aside) The stupidity of this people makes me almost despair.

2nd Convert. True, you have not said in so many words that there are two Gods, but you have said what implies it.

Missionary. Then you have been tempted to reason upon this mystery.

2nd Convert. We, Chinese, are wont to put things together, and to come at truth by comparison. Thus you said there were three persons that were each perfect God, and then you said one of these persons died in one of the countries of the West, a long while ago; and I therefore concluded the present number to be two.

Missionary. Astonishing depravity! O the depths of Satan! It is in vain to reason with these poor benighted creatures. But (addressing the 3rd convert) perverse as your two brethern are, you appear worse than they; what can you possibly mean by answering that there are no Gods?

3rd Convert. I heard you talk of three, but I paid more particular attention to what you said on there being only one. This I could understand; the other I could not; and as my belief never reaches above my understanding (for you know I am no learned Mandarin) I set it down in my mind that there was but one God, and that you take your name of Christian from him.

Missionary. There is something in this; but I am more and more astonished at your answer — "None".

3rd Convert. (Taking up the Swanpan) Here is one. I remove it. There is none.

Missionary. How can this apply?

3rd Convert. Our minds are not like yours in the West, or you would not ask me. You told me again and again, that there never was but one God, that Christ was the true God, and that a nation of merchants living at the head of the Arabian gulf put him to death upon a tree, about eighteen hundred years ago. Believing you, what other answer could I give than "None"?

Missionary. I must pray for you, for you all deny the true faith, and living and dying thus, you will without doubt perish everlastingly.

1st Convert. Cong-foo-tse, our revered master, says that bad temper always turns reason out of doors, and that when men begin to curse, the God Spirit of the universe abandons their hearts.

2nd Convert. You must be angry with yourself and not with us, for you have been teaching us at different times doctrines as contradictory as those of Cong-foo-tse and Buddha. The immortal emperor Sinchong has said that he is not to be numbered with wise men, nor to have a name in the^hall of ancestors, who undertakes a voyage without making up his mind to its purpose, and preparing himself to give a clear and kind answer to the question of a stranger.

3rd Convert. These rebukes are just: but Ter-Whangtee says, in his golden words, that mirth is better than rice. You came, it seems, to bring us a new riddle: but while we thank you, we beg to inform you that Kienlong, our late celestial emperor, has supplied us with a plentiful store, much more entertaining than yours; and when you can read as well as speak our divine language, we recommend to you his delectable history of the Mantchoo Tartar, that pretended to be inspired by the Grand Lama, but could never be made to comprehend the Swanpan.1

1\. The Dialogue is in the form of a satire and was written by Rammohun Roy in his inimitable style to ridicule the Christian cultus of Trinity (God, the Father, God, the Son, Jesus Christ and God, the Holy Ghost) and it served its purpose admirably well. The Serampore missionaries who called Rammohum Roy a hearthen felt so completely nettled that they nearly lost their calm exterior and they became the laughing stock of all intelligent groups in and around Calcutta.
